(i) That Aspee Research Institute. Bombay will maintain

scientific research. 

(ii) That the said Association will furnish annual re¬ 

turns of its scientific research activities to the Pres¬ 
cribed Authority for every financial year in such

for this purpose by 30th April each year. 

(iii) That the said Association v/fil submit to the Pres¬

come-tax.

S.O. 2878.—Hotel Maurya Sheraton (Weli^m Group) S. P. 
Iilarg, New Dclhl-l 10021 were granted on Import Licence No. 
r'/P/2032.‘?49|C|XX/95/H|84 dated 10-4-85 for import of 
Ihrec Nos. lileetronic 3000 STC Teleprinter Terminals for a 
eif valoo of Rs. 97,000 with a validity of 18 months from 
the date of issue. Now the Party has applied for grant 
of a duplicate copy of the Customs Purposes copy of the 
aforesaid Import Licence on the ground that the subject 
copy has been lost. The licensee has fiqnishcd ncceraary 
affidavit according to which the aforesaid Import Licence 
was not registered with any Custom House and was not 
utilised at all and the balance against the licence is Rs. 97,000. 
A declaration has also been incorporated in the affidavit 
to the effect that if the said Import licence is traced or found 
later on, it wilf be returned to the issuing authority. On 
being satisfied that the original Customs Purpose Copy of 
the aforesaid Import 'Licence has been lost, the undersigned 
directs that a Duplicate Customs Purpose copy of Licence 
No. P|P|2032549IC|XX|93|HI84 dated 10-4-85 should be 
issued to the applicant. 1 also, in exercise of the powers 
conferred in sub-clause (d) of clause 9 of the Imports Trade 
(Control) Order 1955. hereby cancel the original Customs 
Purpose copy of the abovg licence.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM AND NATURAL GAS 
New Delhi, ihc 25th July, 1986 

S.O. 2880.—Whereo# it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the trans- 
ort of petroleum from Dabaka G.C.S. to Sarsawni in Gujarat 
tate pipeline should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas 
Commission. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by 
rib-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine¬ 
rals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (3 of 1962), the Central Government hereby de¬ 
clares its intention to acquire the right df user therein ;

Now Delhi, the 22nd July, 1986 

S.O, 2881.—In pursuance of sub-rule (4) of rule 10 of 
the Official language (Use for Official Purposes of the 
Union) Rules, 1976, the Central Government hereby noti¬ 
fies the following offices under the administrative control of 
the Ministry of Food and Civil Supplies {Department of 
Food), the Staff where of have acquired the working know¬ 
ledge of Hindi: 

1. Indian Grain Storage Institute, Jabalpur. 

2. Food and Nutrition Board, New Delhi, 

3. Save Grain Campaign, Bhubaneshwar. 

4. Save Grain Campaign, Bangalore.

1. IS : 3202—1965 Cede of practice 
for climate proofing of electrical

S.O. 1756 dated 1966-05-30 published

14. Now taking it for grriuted that the workman w.is en¬ 
gaged on daily bnsis for bringing water, serving the same 
to the public and staff members in the bank mostly during 
the summer season will it amount to a work in the bankiim 
industry for which it can be said that his engagement was 
in Ihe nature of temporary employee in the banking industry. 

15. It i.s not esiablished that there wok any third po'-t 
of sub staff in Banthra branch of the opposite party occupied 
by Shri Radhey Raman as peon ciun dnflary and Mahcmlr* 
Jha who was peon cum guard. Purther it has not been esta¬ 
blished that he was taken in the leave vacancy of any of 
them. Thirdly the fact that he was being pakl at the rate 
of 5 or 6 per day suggests that he p:iid for the casual natme 
of the work taken by the management. If during the summer 
.season or specific days or period the workman was engaged 
for bringing water and serving drinking water to the stuff 
and public that will not amount to servong in the banking 
industry as in order to show that the man was engaged as 
peon cum water man. It must be shown that bcrldes ;w.rvine 
water to the staff and public which may utrroil amount to 
one of the duties of peon cum water man unless it is shown 
that he performed other duties of peon of the bank beside 
serving water to the public and staff engagement for the dav 
for serving water and taking drinking water to the public 
and the members of the staff fi can not be said the work 
of peon cum water man in the banking indu.stry, a-, it is 
one of the numerous jolis which a peon cum watc. man has 
to be performed in the hanking industry. 

16. In these ciyCutnslHncya and for the reas-jn-, diwujscd 
above, I am not inclined to hold that the workman was en¬ 
gaged for fetching and serving water to the stuff and public, 
termination letter retrenchment compensation, and notice pay 
has to be paid and in the ubBence of all these be has to be 
•beinstated with full back wa®s*,

man w.e.f. 16-7-82 was justified.

8. It has been conceded during the course of argument 
by the workmen representative that workman has no lien to

vants he was liable for transfer from one post to other and 
in that case if from the post of special allowance he could

9. The workman has filed relieving order dated 23-3-85

10. It has been argued for the councel of the workman 
that when junior Sri S. Mukherji was also working as Junior

not on junior showing that he was vilimise for his trade

11. It has been argued on behalf of the management that- 
there is nothing on record to show that General Manager 
received the list of protected workman dated 9-2^85. Rule 
61 of the Indian Civil Service Rules, 1957 lays down that 
every'registered Trade Union shall communicate to tlie 
employer by 30th Apiif every year name address of all the 
protected workman and in case of any dispute the matter 
has to be referred to the RLC(C) or ALC(C) concerned. 
In ciise no objection is raised the employer has to corranu- 
nicate to the union within 15 days that such protected have 


been recognised for a period of one year. Had management 

, yU y b" 5 TahtC. 

objection wiihm I> d''v-i -md mn'f htivc i I'jfd with 

RLC(C> an Ai.CtC) hi the absence of any such objection 
the employer i.e, is the CJeneral Manager in the instant case 
in view Of Rule 2 (G) must have received it in the norma! 
Tourse of actioji. Thus there remains no doubt that the 
vyorkman was protected' workman for the year 1985. In 
s'ievt of the law laid dosvn in case of K. T. Morris reported 
in in AIR 1960 page 675 it was held that the expression 
“workman concerned” could not be limited to such of the 
workman on whose behalf dispute has been raised but also 
against protected W'orkm.in concerned in such dispute in 
view' of Section 33 sub clause fij and (3) of the I.D. Act, 
This contention was upheld in Shalimar Paints Ltd. V/s. 
Third Industrial Tribunal 1974 Lab, I.C. I accordingly hold 
that the workman entitled to move the application. 


12. Thus in view of 33(i) of the I.D. Act his service con¬ 
dition not have been altered by reducing his emoluments 
and exposing him to liabdity of transfer and night duty 
without the express permission of this court when the Indus¬ 
trial Dispute sponsored by the workman as protected work¬ 
man by giving strike notice in proforma-L was pending. 


- 13. In this circun.snances and for the rea.sons discussed 
abpve I Iiold that the change in service condition of the 
workman without express permission of this court was illegal 
and he shall remain on the post and draw all emoluments 
on which he was working on 23-3-85 the day of his transfer 
from the present place of posting. 

14, L therefore gave my award accordingly.
